[Sanctuary of Eyridane and Morana]
The small path ends as the trees part, revealing a small clearing. Sunlight filters down from above, splashing the vibrant green grass and wildflowers. The noise of the central part of the grove all but fades away in this quiet sanctuary. An altar of stone rests in the center of the small clearing of verdant grass. Tangled vines have grown wild across the hard stone and though no markings can be seen, it is obvious that this is a place sacred to the twin goddesses of nature. Overhead the soft warbling of songbirds blends with the chirping crickets below into a sublime symphony of sounds. Small trinkets of adoration and worship have been left at the base of the altar, each one a symbol of devotion to their beloved gods.

The area is illuminated.

[Secluded Grove]
Trees have grown into a natural wall, protecting the clearing from any unwanted visitors. Vines have been woven through the tangled branches of the trees, blocking the rest of the forest from view. Soft grass peppered with patches of clover grows underfoot, and a small, brown rabbit nibbles contentedly on the green stalks.  A small group of sturdy Galirrim has gathered around a small spring of crystal clear water. Enchanting scents of cooking food waft through the air from a painted-wood tavern, closely followed by the sound merry laughter.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Kynea the Zacotl, 1st fox squirrel
In this room are: small spring of crystal clear water

[Overgrown Farmland]
What was once farmland has been taken over by a mass of twisting trees and bushes extending from the woods. [Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

[Urda Town Square Center]

Behind the heavy oak door, the dimly-lit room opens into a spacious tavern full of glass-topped, lacquered wooden tables and cushioned chairs. A beautifully polished bar, carved with star and tree shapes, is set against the northern wall. Behind the bar top, bottles of liquor, kegs of beer, and all manner of alcoholic beverage are lined up, tapped and waiting for patrons. High-backed stools are set against the bar, allowing patrons to mingle with one another. The cacophonous sounds of talking, laughing, and whispering fill the packed room. The hearty aroma of well-cooked food wafts from the kitchen entrance behind the bar. Servers and bartenders move swiftly back and forth from the tavern to the kitchen. A small servant door is visible behind the bar. A T-shaped stage is centered at the eastern wall, and though the barmaids and barkeeps continue to move quickly, refilling drinks or bringing food in and out from the kitchen behind the bar, a reverent silence spreads over the customers as entertainers take the stage. Portraits of various famous adventurers decorate the walls, yet the most pronounced portrait of the owner has been hung behind the bar.

The area is illuminated.

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

The area is illuminated.

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Tavern]
Behind the heavy oak door, the dimly-lit room opens into a spacious tavern full of glass-topped, lacquered wooden tables and cushioned chairs. A beautifully polished bar, carved with star and tree shapes, is set against the northern wall. Behind the bar top, bottles of liquor, kegs of beer, and all manner of alcoholic beverage are lined up, tapped and waiting for patrons. High-backed stools are set against the bar, allowing patrons to mingle with one another. The cacophonous sounds of talking, laughing, and whispering fill the packed room. The hearty aroma of well-cooked food wafts from the kitchen entrance behind the bar. Servers and bartenders move swiftly back and forth from the tavern to the kitchen. A small servant door is visible behind the bar. A T-shaped stage is centered at the eastern wall, and though the barmaids and barkeeps continue to move quickly, refilling drinks or bringing food in and out from the kitchen behind the bar, a reverent silence spreads over the customers as entertainers take the stage. Portraits of various famous adventurers decorate the walls, yet the most pronounced portrait of the owner has been hung behind the bar.

The area is illuminated.

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Tavern]
Behind the heavy oak door, the dimly-lit room opens into a spacious tavern full of glass-topped, lacquered wooden tables and cushioned chairs. A beautifully polished bar, carved with star and tree shapes, is set against the northern wall. Behind the bar top, bottles of liquor, kegs of beer, and all manner of alcoholic beverage are lined up, tapped and waiting for patrons. High-backed stools are set against the bar, allowing patrons to mingle with one another. The cacophonous sounds of talking, laughing, and whispering fill the packed room. The hearty aroma of well-cooked food wafts from the kitchen entrance behind the bar. Servers and bartenders move swiftly back and forth from the tavern to the kitchen. A small servant door is visible behind the bar. A T-shaped stage is centered at the eastern wall, and though the barmaids and barkeeps continue to move quickly, refilling drinks or bringing food in and out from the kitchen behind the bar, a reverent silence spreads over the customers as entertainers take the stage. Portraits of various famous adventurers decorate the walls, yet the most pronounced portrait of the owner has been hung behind the bar.

The area is illuminated.

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: 1st burly, duskwen bouncer, 1st urdaian guardian knight

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Post Office]
The small foyer opens up to a large room filled with aisles of stiff wooden chairs. On the left is a long polished mahogany desk. Inkwells, quill holders, and ever-burning candles sit on its surface. Small, numbered, blue-steel doors, run from floor to ceiling on the right. Customers wait patiently for the stern-looking, Silvarin Postmaster behind the counter. Two doorways with green woolen curtains block off the view to the innermost chamber of the Office. Vibrant activity can be heard within. A beautifully polished bar, carved with star and tree shapes, is set against the northern wall. Behind the bar top, bottles of liquor, kegs of beer, and all manner of alcoholic beverage are lined up, tapped and waiting for patrons. High-backed stools are set against the bar, allowing patrons to mingle with one another. The cacophonous sounds of talking, laughing, and whispering fill the packed room. The hearty aroma of well-cooked food wafts from the kitchen entrance behind the bar. Servers and bartenders move swiftly back and forth from the tavern to the kitchen. A small servant door is visible behind the bar. A T-shaped stage is centered at the eastern wall, and though the barmaids and barkeeps continue to move quickly, refilling drinks or bringing food in and out from the kitchen behind the bar, a reverent silence spreads over the customers as entertainers take the stage. Portraits of various famous adventurers decorate the walls, yet the most pronounced portrait of the owner has been hung behind the bar.

The area is illuminated.

Furrows in the road,caused by years of hooves and wheels, have caused some travelers to forge a new path close to the fence. A dilapidated old house sits like an eyesore along the northern edge of the road.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: northwest, west, splintered doorway

w

[Grove]
Pecan and black walnut trees compose a small grove bordered by wide, open fields. From the southern side of the road, the river roars as the water rushes over a particularly rocky spot. A fine mist washes over the road, leaving a glossy sheen to the tall grass.

The area is illuminated.

[Road]
An old shack, once the home of the ferryman, sits to the north side of the road. Not needed since the bridge was built, the shack is in disrepair to the point of being uninhabitable. Small cedar trees, descendants of the larger trees growing near the river bank, block the only entrance to the dilapidated hovel. A stone bridge which leads southwest is here.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: southwest, east, bridge

sw

[Bridge]
The bridge over the river is a marvel of engineering. Columns made entirely of river stones support the massive cypress beams, each hewn from a single tree. Broad oak planks span the space between each beam with only the smallest of gaps between each plank. Since its construction, the bridge has become a favorite fishing spot for many a young boy looking to escape his chores. Spotted jewelweed and monkey flowers grow along each of the river's banks.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: southwest, northeast

sw

[Muddy Road]
The land between Fallow's Lake and the river is quite marshy. Cypress trees and ferns are the predominant vegetation, with marsh cinquefoil throwing splotches of red and purple to break up the drab green and brown scenery. The road, constructed from dirt dredged from the marsh itself, is slightly elevated over its boggy surroundings. A large stone bridge spans northeast across the river.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: southwest, northeast, bridge

sw

[Marshes Edge]
The road follows the edge of the marsh, acting as a dividing line between the wetlands on one side and the open expanse of the plains on the other. Thick reeds and even thicker grass hide any natural landmarks this area may hold.  Some animal footprints can be seen in the muddy ground.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: southwest, northeast

w

[Wetlands]
The edge of the wetlands is clearly marked by the tall rushes that come right up to the roadside. On the north side, the grasslands extend outward to the horizon and beyond. The sounds of buzzing insects hums softly; the occasional firefly peeps out of the safe haven of the tall grass.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, northeast

sw

[Marsh]
Following the natural course of the marsh, the road curves slightly. The square-stemmed monkey flowers, with their decidedly violet color, stick their heads out here and there amongst the rushes. Cattails which line the road bend drastically due to the weight of their blooms.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: southwest, east

sw

[Field]
Starved aster and agrimony grow in the open fields to the north side of the road. The white and yellow flowers seem to float contentedly on the golden tide of the grasslands. The marsh boasts of its color as the reds and purples of the cinquefoils stand proudly among the rushes.

The area is illuminated.

[Hidden Shadowy Alcove]
The air in this narrow room seems compressed and tight, almost as if it were made of thousands of tiny needles ready to pierce flesh and bone. The darkness is thick and oppressive, and almost tangible. It seems as if the blackness is not merely the lack of luminance, but something far more sinister. The floor is jagged and treacherous, one wrong step and it could end a life. The walls are rocky and uneven, and thin streams of acidic water run down the stone, slowly eating away at the rock with loud hissing pops. Whatever is perverting the cathedral on the surface certainly makes its lair right here.

The area is illuminated.

[Twisted Central Bell Tower]
The air grows colder and the warmth of the sun feels like only a distant memory in this room. The walls of this upside-down spire are opened into inverted arches, just like the true tower, but here they show a view of a massive cavern. An immense jungle of towering mushrooms reach toward the cavern ceiling and piles of moss glow softly in the darkness, providing a weak but constant light. In the far distance is a towering structure clothed in shadow. A magical shield glimmers weakly in the space of the arch and a sudden arc of electricity in the rippling air suggests that it would be unwise to touch the shield. It might look open, but something nearly invisible and highly dangerous blocks the way into the mysterious underground world.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: 1st deformed chimera
Obvious exits are: west, east, dark stone spiraling steps

The floor is jagged and treacherous, one wrong step and it could end a life. The walls are rocky and uneven, and thin streams of acidic water run down the stone, slowly eating away at the rock with loud hissing pops. Whatever is perverting the cathedral on the surface certainly makes its lair right here.

The area is illuminated.

[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

[Merchant Square]
A small sign has been set in front of the nearby store fronts, listing the nearby shops: Tillie's Trinkets, Liddie's Lights, and the Soles of Urda. The north side of the the square elegantly frames a beautiful multicolored storefront. Small and manicured green and red shrubbery have taken purchase beside a impeccably clean glass door. In the front window what appears to be swirling and swaying light spells     "L i d d i e s". Directly south of the storefront, the square displays a deteriorating wooden shop. White painted wood peels with age and piles of dirt accumulate along the shop front. A ragged sign which hangs from only one chain above the cracked and knotty wooden door reads "Tillie's Trinkets and Treasures." A newly varnished storefront with a beaded glass curtain sits next to Tillie's.

[Western Cross Road]
Wagon marks mar the uneven cobbled road from so much use. Shops spring up on either side of the road, their owners gesturing for patrons to come in and try their new wares. A large Galirrim, towering above the other passersby, lumbers towards the town square in shreds of clothing. Other pedestrians make way for the Galirrim and go about their business, never giving him a second thought. The diversity of races walking down the road is astounding.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

[Western Cross Road]
A red brick storefront rises up two stories along the cobblestone road. Patrons bustle in and out of the shop which displays beautiful, one-of-a-kind, handcrafted clothing pieces and jewelry. Sounds of laughter and drinks being poured can be heard from inside as well, muffled behind the walls of the store. A gentleman usher politely opens the frosted glass double-doors for patrons as they pass through. Delicate silver stars have been filigreed into the glass of the doors, creating a backdrop for words shaped in gold thread stitch-lettering, "The Lonely Moon: Stars Club."

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, open pair of frosted glass double doors

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

The area is illuminated.

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Tavern]
Behind the heavy oak door, the dimly-lit room opens into a spacious tavern full of glass-topped, lacquered wooden tables and cushioned chairs. A beautifully polished bar, carved with star and tree shapes, is set against the northern wall. Behind the bar top, bottles of liquor, kegs of beer, and all manner of alcoholic beverage are lined up, tapped and waiting for patrons. High-backed stools are set against the bar, allowing patrons to mingle with one another. The cacophonous sounds of talking, laughing, and whispering fill the packed room. The hearty aroma of well-cooked food wafts from the kitchen entrance behind the bar. Servers and bartenders move swiftly back and forth from the tavern to the kitchen. A small servant door is visible behind the bar. A T-shaped stage is centered at the eastern wall, and though the barmaids and barkeeps continue to move quickly, refilling drinks or bringing food in and out from the kitchen behind the bar, a reverent silence spreads over the customers as entertainers take the stage. Portraits of various famous adventurers decorate the walls, yet the most pronounced portrait of the owner has been hung behind the bar.

The area is illuminated.

[Travers Town Square]
Small marble benches are scattered neatly about the square welcoming travelers to take a seat or to possibly empty pebbles from their boots. Cobblestone streets decorate the square alternating from browns to reds in a random pattern. Small statues of the world's races stand behind each bench as if standing sentinel. Tall lamp posts with wrought iron lanterns look down on the neat and clean streets. An occasional waste engineer wanders through, sweeping up dirt and trash into tin dustpans. Tucked away in a corner is an old door leading to a rundown store. The loud echoing of dripping water can be heard coming from a metal grate which is recessed into the street.

The area is illuminated.
